<div id="p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown mw-portlet mw-portlet-lang"  >
	<input type="checkbox" id="p-lang-btn-checkbox" role="button" aria-haspopup="true" data-event-name="ui.dropdown-p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown-checkbox mw-interlanguage-selector" aria-label="Go to an article in another language. Available in 249 languages"   >

	</label>

in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="&#39;w&#39; in &#39;wind&#39;">w</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/d\/d4\/En-Bostwana-pronunciation.ogg\/En-Bostwana-pronunciation.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;En-Bostwana-pronunciation.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/d/d4/En-Bostwana-pronunciation.ogg/En-Bostwana-pronunciation.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:En-Bostwana-pronunciation.ogg" title="File:En-Bostwana-pronunciation.ogg">i</a></sup></span>, <span style="font-size:85%;">also</span> <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><small><a href="/wiki/British_English" title="British English">UK</a>: </small><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ʊ/: &#39;u&#39; in &#39;push&#39;">ʊ</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span></span>-,<span class="wrap"> </span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ʊ/: &#39;u&#39; in &#39;push&#39;">ʊ</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/tʃ/: &#39;ch&#39; in &#39;China&#39;">tʃ</span><span title="&#39;w&#39; in &#39;wind&#39;">w</span></span>-/</a></span></span><sup id="cite_ref-Collins_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Collins-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>), officially the <b>Republic of Botswana</b> (<a href="/wiki/Setswana" class="mw-redirect" title="Setswana">Setswana</a>: <i lang="tn">Lefatshe la Botswana</i>, <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="tn-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA" title="Help:IPA">[lɪˈfatsʰɪ la bʊˈtswana]</a></span>), is a <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked country</a> in <a href="/wiki/Southern_Africa" title="Southern Africa">Southern Africa</a>. Botswana is topographically flat, with approximately 70 per cent of its territory being the <a href="/wiki/Kalahari_Desert" title="Kalahari Desert">Kalahari Desert</a>. It is bordered by <a href="/wiki/South_Africa" title="South Africa">South Africa</a> to the south and southeast, <a href="/wiki/Namibia" title="Namibia">Namibia</a> to the west and north, and <a href="/wiki/Zimbabwe" title="Zimbabwe">Zimbabwe</a> to the northeast. It is connected by the <a href="/wiki/Kazungula_Bridge" title="Kazungula Bridge">Kazungula Bridge</a><sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> to <a href="/wiki/Zambia" title="Zambia">Zambia</a>, across <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_territories_by_land_borders#Superlatives" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries and territories by land borders">the world's shortest border between two countries</a>.
</p><p>A country of slightly over 2.3 million people,<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Botswana is one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population_density" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by population density">most sparsely populated</a> countries in the world. It is essentially the nation state of the <a href="/wiki/Tswana_people" title="Tswana people">Tswana</a>, who make up 79% of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> About 11.6 per cent of the population lives in the capital and largest city, <a href="/wiki/Gaborone" title="Gaborone">Gaborone</a>. Formerly one of the world's poorest countries—with a GDP per capita of about US$70 per year in the late 1960s—it has since transformed itself into an upper-middle-income country, with one of the world's fastest-growing economies.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Modern-day humans first inhabited the country over 200,000 years ago. The Tswana ethnic group were descended mainly from <a href="/wiki/Bantu_peoples" title="Bantu peoples">Bantu-speaking tribes</a> who migrated southward of Africa to modern Botswana around 600 AD, living in tribal enclaves as farmers and herders. In 1885, the <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British</a> colonised the area and declared a <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">protectorate</a> under the name of <a href="/wiki/Bechuanaland_Protectorate" title="Bechuanaland Protectorate">Bechuanaland</a>. As <a href="/wiki/Decolonization_of_Africa" class="mw-redirect" title="Decolonization of Africa">decolonisation occurred</a>, Bechuanaland became an independent <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Commonwealth republic">Commonwealth republic</a> under its current name on 30 September 1966.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Since then, it has been a <a href="/wiki/Representative_republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Representative republic">representative republic</a>, with a consistent record of uninterrupted democratic <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_Botswana" title="Elections in Botswana">elections</a> and the lowest perceived corruption ranking in Africa since at least 1998.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The economy is dominated by <a href="/wiki/Mining_industry_of_Botswana" title="Mining industry of Botswana">mining</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Botswana" title="Tourism in Botswana">tourism</a>. Botswana has a GDP (<a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">purchasing power parity</a>) per capita of about $18,113 as of 2021<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, one of the highest in the subsaharan Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> Botswana is the world's biggest diamond producing country. Its relatively high <a href="/wiki/Gross_National_Income_(GNI)_per_capita" class="mw-redirect" title="Gross National Income (GNI) per capita">gross national income per capita</a> (by some estimates the fourth-largest in Africa) gives the country a relatively high <a href="/wiki/Standard_of_living" title="Standard of living">standard of living</a> and the third-highest <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a> of continental <a href="/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africa" title="Sub-Saharan Africa">Sub-Saharan Africa</a> (after Gabon and South Africa).<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2022_components_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2022_components-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>  Botswana is the first African country to host <a href="/wiki/Forbes_30_Under_30" title="Forbes 30 Under 30">Forbes 30 Under 30</a><sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/2017_Netball_World_Youth_Cup" title="2017 Netball World Youth Cup">2017 Netball World Youth Cup</a>.
</p><p>Botswana is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Southern_African_Customs_Union" title="Southern African Customs Union">Southern African Customs Union</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Southern_African_Development_Community" title="Southern African Development Community">Southern African Development Community</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>. The country has been adversely affected by the <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS_in_Botswana" title="HIV/AIDS in Botswana">HIV/AIDS epidemic</a>. In 2002, Botswana became the first country to offer anti-retroviral drugs (ARVs) to help combat the epidemic.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Despite the launch of programs to make treatment available and to educate the populace about the epidemic,<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> the number of people with AIDS rose from 290,000 in 2005 to 320,000 in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-Gap_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gap-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: A20">&#58;&#8202;A20&#8202;</span></sup> As of 2014<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, Botswana had the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_HIV/AIDS_adult_prevalence_rate" title="List of countries by HIV/AIDS adult prevalence rate">third-highest prevalence rate</a> for HIV/AIDS, with roughly 20% of the population infected.<sup id="cite_ref-Avert_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Avert-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> However, in recent years the country has made strides in combatting HIV/AIDS, with efforts being made to provide proper treatment and lower the rate of mother-to-child transmission.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The country's name means "Land of the <a href="/wiki/Tswana_people" title="Tswana people">Tswana</a>", referring to the dominant <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Botswana" title="Ethnic groups in Botswana">ethnic group in Botswana</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The Constitution of Botswana recognizes a homogeneous Tswana state.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The term <i>Batswana</i> was originally applied to the Tswana, which is still the case.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> However, it has also come to be used generally as a <a href="/wiki/Demonym" title="Demonym">demonym</a> for all citizens of Botswana.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Botswana" title="History of Botswana">History of Botswana</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_history">Early history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Early history">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Two_Rhino.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Two_Rhino.jpg/220px-Two_Rhino.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Two_Rhino.jpg/330px-Two_Rhino.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Two_Rhino.jpg/440px-Two_Rhino.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="1339" /></a><figcaption>The 'Two Rhino' painting at <a href="/wiki/Tsodilo" title="Tsodilo">Tsodilo</a>, a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Site</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Archaeological digs demonstrate that hominids lived in Botswana for around two million years.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Stone tools and fauna remains have shown that all areas of the country were inhabited at least 400,000 years ago.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>In October 2019, researchers reported that Botswana was the birthplace of all <a href="/wiki/Anatomically_modern_humans" class="mw-redirect" title="Anatomically modern humans">modern humans</a> about 200,000 years ago.<sup id="cite_ref-NAT-20191028_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NAT-20191028-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-SA-20191028_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SA-20191028-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Evidence left by modern humans, such as cave paintings, is about 73,000 years old.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> The earliest known inhabitants of southern Africa are thought to have been the forebears of present-day <a href="/wiki/San_people" title="San people">San</a> ("Bushmen") and <a href="/wiki/Khoi" class="mw-redirect" title="Khoi">Khoi</a> peoples. Both groups speak <a href="/wiki/Click_consonant" title="Click consonant">click languages</a> from the small <a href="/wiki/Khoe%E2%80%93Kwadi_languages" title="Khoe–Kwadi languages">Khoe-Kwadi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kx%CA%BCa_languages" title="Kxʼa languages">Kx’a</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tuu_languages" title="Tuu languages">Tuu</a> families whose members hunted, gathered, and traded over long distances. When cattle were first introduced about 2000 years ago into southern Africa, pastoralism became a major feature of the economy, since the region had large grasslands free of <a href="/wiki/Tsetse_fly" title="Tsetse fly">tsetse flies</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{display:flex;flex-direction:row;clear:left;flex-wrap:wrap;width:100%;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{margin:1px;float:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .theader{clear:both;font-weight:bold;text-align:center;align-self:center;background-color:transparent;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{background-color:transparent}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-left{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-right{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-center{text-align:center}@media all and (max-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbinner{width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;max-width:none!important;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{justify-content:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{float:none!important;max-width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle .thumbcaption{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow>.thumbcaption{text-align:center}}</style><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:204px;max-width:204px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Domboshaba_stone_wall_3.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Domboshaba_stone_wall_3.jpg/200px-Domboshaba_stone_wall_3.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="133" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Domboshaba_stone_wall_3.jpg/300px-Domboshaba_stone_wall_3.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Domboshaba_stone_wall_3.jpg/400px-Domboshaba_stone_wall_3.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" /></a></span></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:The_plate_that_was_used_in_the_past_at_Domboshaba_monument.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/The_plate_that_was_used_in_the_past_at_Domboshaba_monument.jpg/200px-The_plate_that_was_used_in_the_past_at_Domboshaba_monument.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="133" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/The_plate_that_was_used_in_the_past_at_Domboshaba_monument.jpg/300px-The_plate_that_was_used_in_the_past_at_Domboshaba_monument.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/The_plate_that_was_used_in_the_past_at_Domboshaba_monument.jpg/400px-The_plate_that_was_used_in_the_past_at_Domboshaba_monument.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/Domboshaba" title="Domboshaba">Domboshaba</a> Ruins Stone Wall (top) and clay pottery plate (bottom)</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>It is unclear when <a href="/wiki/Bantu_languages" title="Bantu languages">Bantu</a>-speaking peoples first moved into the country from the north, although AD 600 seems to be a consensus estimate. In that era the ancestors of the modern-day <a href="/wiki/Kalanga_people" title="Kalanga people">Kalanga</a> moved into what is now the north-eastern areas of the country. These proto-Kalanga were closely connected to states in <a href="/wiki/Zimbabwe" title="Zimbabwe">Zimbabwe</a> as well as to the <a href="/wiki/Mapungubwe" class="mw-redirect" title="Mapungubwe">Mapungubwe</a> state and the notable of these was <a href="/wiki/Domboshaba" title="Domboshaba">Domboshaba</a> ruins, a cultural and heritage site in Botswana originally occupied towards the end of the Great Zimbabwe period (1250–1450 AD), with stone walls that have an average height of 1.8 metres. The site is a respected place for the people living in the region and it is believed that the chief lived on the top of the hill together with his helpers or assistants. These states, located outside of current Botswana's borders, appear to have kept massive herds of cattle in what is now the Central District—apparently at numbers approaching modern cattle density.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> This massive cattle-raising complex prospered until 1300 AD or so and seems to have regressed following the collapse of Mapungubwe. During this era the first Tswana-speaking groups, the <a href="/wiki/Bakgalagadi" class="mw-redirect" title="Bakgalagadi">Bakgalagadi</a>, moved into the southern areas of the <a href="/wiki/Kalahari" class="mw-redirect" title="Kalahari">Kalahari</a>. All these various peoples were connected to trade routes that ran via the Limpopo River to the Indian Ocean, and trade goods from Asia such as beads made their way to Botswana, most likely in exchange for ivory, gold and <a href="/wiki/Rhinoceros_horn" class="mw-redirect" title="Rhinoceros horn">rhinoceros horn</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Toutswemogala_Hill" title="Toutswemogala Hill">Toutswemogala Hill</a> Iron Age Settlement's radio-carbon dates for this settlement range from 7th to late 19th century AD indicating occupation of more than one thousand years. The hill was part of the formation of early states in Southern Africa with cattle keeping as major source of economy. Toutswe settlement include house-floors, large heaps of vitrified cow-dung and burials while the outstanding structure is the stone wall. There are large tracts of centaurs ciliaris, a type of grass which has come to be associated with cattle-keeping settlements in South, Central Africa. Around 700 A.D., the <a href="/wiki/Toutswe" class="mw-redirect" title="Toutswe">Toutswe</a> people moved westward into Botswana and began an agricultural and pastoral land tenure system based on <a href="/wiki/Sorghum" title="Sorghum">sorghum</a> and <a href="/wiki/Millet" title="Millet">millet</a>, and domesticated stock, respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-Hall_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hall-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> The site was situated in the centre of a broader cultural area in Eastern Botswana and shares many commonalities with other archaeological sites of this region, in both ceramic production styles and also time frames inhabited. Large structures were observed that contained vitrified remains of animal dung, leading to the theory that these were animal enclosures and that Toutswemogala Hill was thus a major centre of animal husbandry in the region.<sup id="cite_ref-Hall_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hall-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>However, agriculture also played a vital role in the longevity of Toutswemogala Hill's extended occupation, as many grain storage structures have also been found on the site. Many different stratified layers of housing floors further signal continuous occupation over hundreds of years. The arrival of the ancestors of the Tswana-speakers who came to control the region has yet to be dated precisely. Members of the <a href="/wiki/Bakwena" class="mw-redirect" title="Bakwena">Bakwena</a>, a chieftaincy under a legendary leader named Kgabo II, made their way into the southern Kalahari by AD 1500, at the latest, and his people drove the Bakgalagadi inhabitants west into the desert. Over the years, several offshoots of the Bakwena moved into adjoining territories. The <a href="/wiki/Bangwaketse" title="Bangwaketse">Bangwaketse</a> occupied areas to the west, while the <a href="/wiki/Bangwato" class="mw-redirect" title="Bangwato">Bangwato</a> moved northeast into formerly Kalanga areas.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Not long afterwards, a Bangwato offshoot known as the Batawana migrated into the Okavango Delta, probably in the 1790s.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Effects_of_the_Mfecane_and_Batswana-Boer_Wars">Effects of the Mfecane and Batswana-Boer Wars</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Effects of the Mfecane and Batswana-Boer Wars">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mfecane" title="Mfecane">Mfecane</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kapstaaten_1905.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Kapstaaten_1905.png/220px-Kapstaaten_1905.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="224" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Kapstaaten_1905.png/330px-Kapstaaten_1905.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Kapstaaten_1905.png/440px-Kapstaaten_1905.png 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="1016" /></a><figcaption>German map of 1905 still showing the undivided Bechuanaland area</figcaption></figure>
<p>The first written records relating to modern-day Botswana appear in 1824. What these records show is that the Bangwaketse had become the predominant power in the region. Under the rule of Makaba II, the Bangwaketse kept vast herds of cattle in well-protected desert areas, and used their military prowess to raid their neighbours.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> Other chiefdoms in the area, by this time, had capitals of 10,000 or so and were fairly prosperous.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> This equilibrium came to end during the <a href="/wiki/Mfecane" title="Mfecane">Mfecane</a> period, 1823–1843, when a succession of invading peoples from South Africa entered the country. Although the Bangwaketse were able to defeat the invading <a href="/wiki/Makololo_tribe" class="mw-redirect" title="Makololo tribe">Bakololo</a> in 1826, over time all the major chiefdoms in Botswana were attacked, weakened, and impoverished. The Bakololo and <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ndebele_people" title="Northern Ndebele people">AmaNdebele</a> raided repeatedly and took large numbers of cattle, women, and children from the Batswana—most of whom were driven into the desert or sanctuary areas such as hilltops and caves. Only after 1843, when the Amandebele moved into western Zimbabwe, did this threat subside.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sechele_Gustav_Fritsch_1865.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Sechele_Gustav_Fritsch_1865.jpg/170px-Sechele_Gustav_Fritsch_1865.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Sechele_Gustav_Fritsch_1865.jpg/255px-Sechele_Gustav_Fritsch_1865.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Sechele_Gustav_Fritsch_1865.jpg/340px-Sechele_Gustav_Fritsch_1865.jpg 2x" data-file-width="592" data-file-height="790" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Sechele_I" title="Sechele I">Sechele I</a> who led a Batswana Merafe Coalition against <a href="/wiki/Boers" title="Boers">Boers</a> in 1852</figcaption></figure>
<p>During the 1840s and 1850s trade with <a href="/wiki/Cape_Colony" title="Cape Colony">Cape Colony</a>-based merchants opened up and enabled the <a href="/wiki/Batswana" class="mw-redirect" title="Batswana">Batswana</a> chiefdoms to rebuild. The <a href="/wiki/Bakwena" class="mw-redirect" title="Bakwena">Bakwena</a>, Bangwaketse, Bangwato and Batawana cooperated to control the lucrative ivory trade and then used the proceeds to import horses and guns, which in turn enabled them to establish control over what is now Botswana. This process was largely complete by 1880, and thus the Bushmen, the Kalanga, the Bakgalagadi, and other current minorities were subjugated by the Batswana.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The earliest known map of Botswana dates from 1849, drawn by <a href="/wiki/David_Livingstone" title="David Livingstone">David Livingstone</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following the <a href="/wiki/Great_Trek" title="Great Trek">Great Trek</a>, <a href="/wiki/Afrikaners" title="Afrikaners">Afrikaners</a> from the Cape Colony established themselves on the borders of Botswana in the <a href="/wiki/South_African_Republic" title="South African Republic">Transvaal</a>. In 1852 a coalition of <a href="/wiki/Tswana_people" title="Tswana people">Tswana</a> chiefdoms led by <a href="/wiki/Sechele_I" title="Sechele I">Sechele I</a> defeated Afrikaner incursions at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Dimawe" title="Battle of Dimawe">Battle of Dimawe</a> and, after about eight years of intermittent tensions and hostilities, eventually came to a peace agreement in Potchefstroom in 1860. From that point on, the modern-day border between South Africa and Botswana was agreed on, and the Afrikaners and Batswana traded and worked together comparatively peacefully.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1884 Batawana, a northern based Tswana clan's cavalry under the command of Kgosi Moremi fought and defeated the Ndebele's invasion of northern Botswana at the Battle of Khutiyabasadi. This blow to the larger invading Ndebele force signalled the start of the collapse of the Ndebele Kingdom in Zimbabwe and helped galvanise Tswana speaking authority of the area now making part of northern Botswana.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Due to newly peaceful conditions, trade thrived between 1860 and 1880. Taking advantage of this were Christian <a href="/wiki/Missionary" title="Missionary">missionaries</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Lutherans" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutherans">Lutherans</a> and the <a href="/wiki/London_Missionary_Society" title="London Missionary Society">London Missionary Society</a> both became established in the country by 1856. By 1880 every major village had a resident missionary, and their influence slowly became felt. <a href="/wiki/Khama_III" title="Khama III">Khama III</a> (reigned 1875–1923) was the first of the Tswana chiefs to make Christianity a state religion and changed a great deal of Tswana customary law as a result. Christianity became the de facto official religion in all the chiefdoms by World War I.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Colonialism_and_the_Bechuanaland_Protectorate">Colonialism and the Bechuanaland Protectorate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Colonialism and the Bechuanaland Protectorate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Bechuanaland_Protectorate" title="Bechuanaland Protectorate">Bechuanaland Protectorate</a></div><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Botswana,_Gaborone_3_Dikgosi_Monument.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Botswana%2C_Gaborone_3_Dikgosi_Monument.jpg/220px-Botswana%2C_Gaborone_3_Dikgosi_Monument.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Botswana%2C_Gaborone_3_Dikgosi_Monument.jpg/330px-Botswana%2C_Gaborone_3_Dikgosi_Monument.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Botswana%2C_Gaborone_3_Dikgosi_Monument.jpg/440px-Botswana%2C_Gaborone_3_Dikgosi_Monument.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="1836" /></a><figcaption>3 Dikgosi Monument: <a href="/wiki/Khama_III" title="Khama III">Khama III</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sebele_I" title="Sebele I">Sebele I</a> &amp; <a href="/wiki/Bathoen_I" title="Bathoen I">Bathoen I</a> who negotiated a Protectorate</figcaption></figure>
<p>During the <a href="/wiki/Scramble_for_Africa" title="Scramble for Africa">Scramble for Africa</a> the territory of Botswana was coveted by both the <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">German Empire</a> and Britain. During the <a href="/wiki/Berlin_Conference" title="Berlin Conference">Berlin Conference</a>, Britain decided to annex Botswana in order to safeguard the Road to the North and thus connect the Cape Colony to its territories further north. It unilaterally annexed Tswana territories in January 1885 and then sent the <a href="/wiki/Warren_Expedition" class="mw-redirect" title="Warren Expedition">Warren Expedition</a> north to consolidate control over the area and convince the chiefs to accept British overrule. Despite their misgivings, they eventually acquiesced to this <i><a href="/wiki/Glossary_of_French_expressions_in_English#Fait_accompli" class="mw-redirect" title="Glossary of French expressions in English">fait accompli</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1890 areas north of 22 degrees were added to the new <a href="/wiki/Bechuanaland_Protectorate" title="Bechuanaland Protectorate">Bechuanaland Protectorate</a>. During the 1890s the new territory was divided into eight different reserves, with fairly small amounts of land being left as freehold for white <a href="/wiki/Settlers" class="mw-redirect" title="Settlers">settlers</a>. During the early 1890s, the British government decided to hand over the Bechuanaland Protectorate to the <a href="/wiki/British_South_Africa_Company" title="British South Africa Company">British South Africa Company</a>. This plan, which was well on its way to fruition despite the entreaties of Tswana leaders who toured England in protest, was eventually foiled by the failure of the <a href="/wiki/Jameson_Raid" title="Jameson Raid">Jameson Raid</a> in January 1896.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:1960_6d_Bechuanaland_Protectorate_stamp.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/1960_6d_Bechuanaland_Protectorate_stamp.jpg/220px-1960_6d_Bechuanaland_Protectorate_stamp.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/1960_6d_Bechuanaland_Protectorate_stamp.jpg/330px-1960_6d_Bechuanaland_Protectorate_stamp.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/1960_6d_Bechuanaland_Protectorate_stamp.jpg/440px-1960_6d_Bechuanaland_Protectorate_stamp.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1172" data-file-height="856" /></a><figcaption>Postage stamp of British-ruled <a href="/wiki/Bechuanaland_Protectorate" title="Bechuanaland Protectorate">Bechuanaland</a> from 1960</figcaption></figure>
<p>When the <a href="/wiki/Union_of_South_Africa" title="Union of South Africa">Union of South Africa</a> was formed in 1910 from the main British colonies in the region, the High Commission Territories—the Bechuanaland Protectorate, <a href="/wiki/Basutoland" title="Basutoland">Basutoland</a> (now <a href="/wiki/Lesotho" title="Lesotho">Lesotho</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Swaziland" class="mw-redirect" title="Swaziland">Swaziland</a> (now <a href="/wiki/Eswatini" title="Eswatini">Eswatini</a>)—were not included, but provision was made for their later incorporation. However, the UK began to consult with their inhabitants as to their wishes. Although successive South African governments sought to have the territories transferred to their jurisdiction, the UK kept delaying; subsequently, it never occurred. The election of the <a href="/wiki/National_Party_(South_Africa)" title="National Party (South Africa)">Nationalist</a> government in 1948, which instituted <a href="/wiki/Apartheid" title="Apartheid">apartheid</a>, and South Africa's withdrawal from the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth</a> in 1961, ended any prospect of the UK or these territories agreeing to incorporation into South Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>An expansion of British central authority and the evolution of native government resulted in the 1920 establishment of two advisory councils to represent both Africans and Europeans.<sup id="cite_ref-EISA_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EISA-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> The African Council consisted of the eight heads of the Tswana tribes and some elected members.<sup id="cite_ref-EISA_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EISA-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Proclamations in 1934 regulated tribal rule and powers. A European-African advisory council was formed in 1951, and the 1961 constitution established a consultative legislative council.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence">Independence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Independence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Independence_Day_(Botswana)" title="Independence Day (Botswana)">Independence Day (Botswana)</a></div>
<p>In June 1964, the United Kingdom accepted proposals for a democratic self-government in Botswana. An independence conference was held in London in February 1966.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> The seat of government was moved in 1965 from <a href="/wiki/Mahikeng" class="mw-redirect" title="Mahikeng">Mahikeng</a> in South Africa, to the newly established <a href="/wiki/Gaborone" title="Gaborone">Gaborone</a>, which is located near Botswana's border with South Africa. Based on the 1965 constitution, the country held its first general elections under universal suffrage and gained independence on 30 September 1966.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Seretse_Khama" title="Seretse Khama">Seretse Khama</a>, a leader in the independence movement and the legitimate claimant to the <a href="/wiki/Bamangwato" class="mw-redirect" title="Bamangwato">Ngwato</a> chiefship, was elected as the first president, and subsequently re-elected twice.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Queen Elizabeth II">Queen Elizabeth II</a>, accompanied by <a href="/wiki/Prince_Philip,_Duke_of_Edinburgh" title="Prince Philip, Duke of Edinburgh">Prince Philip, Duke of Edinburgh</a> and her son <a href="/wiki/Prince_Andrew,_Duke_of_York" title="Prince Andrew, Duke of York">Prince Andrew, Duke of York</a>, arrived in Botswana during the fourth-leg tour of Africa on 25-27 July 1979. During their visit, they were greeted by tribal dancers and a 21-gun salute. 
</p><p>Khama died in office in 1980. The presidency passed to the sitting vice-president, <a href="/wiki/Quett_Masire" title="Quett Masire">Quett Masire</a>, who was elected in his own right in 1984 and re-elected in 1989 and 1994. Masire retired from office in 1998. He was succeeded by <a href="/wiki/Festus_Mogae" title="Festus Mogae">Festus Mogae</a>, who was elected in his own right in 1999 and re-elected in 2004. The presidency passed in 2008 to <a href="/wiki/Ian_Khama" title="Ian Khama">Ian Khama</a> (son of the first president), who had been serving as Mogae's vice-president since resigning his position in 1998 as Commander of the <a href="/wiki/Botswana_Defence_Force" title="Botswana Defence Force">Botswana Defence Force</a> to take up this civilian role. On 1 April 2018 <a href="/wiki/Mokgweetsi_Masisi" title="Mokgweetsi Masisi">Mokgweetsi Eric Keabetswe Masisi</a> was sworn in as the fifth president of Botswana, succeeding Ian Khama. He represents the <a href="/wiki/Botswana_Democratic_Party" title="Botswana Democratic Party">Botswana Democratic Party</a>, which has also won a majority in every parliamentary election since independence. All the previous presidents have also represented the same party.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A long-running dispute over the northern border with <a href="/wiki/Namibia" title="Namibia">Namibia</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Caprivi_Strip" title="Caprivi Strip">Caprivi Strip</a> was the subject of a ruling by the <a href="/wiki/International_Court_of_Justice" title="International Court of Justice">International Court of Justice</a> in December 1999. It ruled that <a href="/wiki/Kasikili_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Kasikili Island">Kasikili Island</a> belongs to Botswana.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2014, the <a href="/wiki/Okavango_Delta" title="Okavango Delta">Okavango Delta</a> of Botswana, the largest inland delta in the world, was inscribed as the 1000th <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a>.
</p><p>In the 1970s, Botswana held a reputation of being one the world's principal producers of diamonds.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> This reputation has held into the modern day as <a href="/wiki/Mining_industry_of_Botswana#Diamonds" title="Mining industry of Botswana">Botswana's diamond mining industry</a> is among the world's largest.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Botswana's <a href="/wiki/Jwaneng_diamond_mine" title="Jwaneng diamond mine">Jwaneng mine</a> in particular is the world's richest.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Botswana" title="Geography of Botswana">Geography of Botswana</a>, <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Botswana" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate of Botswana">Climate of Botswana</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Climate_Change_in_Botswana" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate Change in Botswana">Climate Change in Botswana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Botswana_map_of_K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Botswana_map_of_K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification.svg/220px-Botswana_map_of_K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Botswana_map_of_K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification.svg/330px-Botswana_map_of_K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Botswana_map_of_K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification.svg/440px-Botswana_map_of_K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1900" data-file-height="1900" /></a><figcaption>Botswana map of <a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification" title="Köppen climate classification">Köppen climate classification</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>At 581,730&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (224,607&#160;sq&#160;mi) Botswana is the world's 48th-largest country. It is similar in size to Madagascar or France. It lies between latitudes 17° and 27° south, and longitudes 20° and 30° east.
</p><p>Botswana is predominantly flat, tending towards gently rolling <a href="/wiki/Plateau" title="Plateau">tableland</a>. Botswana is dominated by the <a href="/wiki/Kalahari_Desert" title="Kalahari Desert">Kalahari Desert</a>, which covers up to 70% of its land surface. The <a href="/wiki/Okavango_Delta" title="Okavango Delta">Okavango Delta</a>, one of the world's largest inland <a href="/wiki/River_deltas" class="mw-redirect" title="River deltas">river deltas</a>, is in the north-west. The <a href="/wiki/Makgadikgadi_Pan" title="Makgadikgadi Pan">Makgadikgadi Pan</a>, a large <a href="/wiki/Salt_pan_(geology)" title="Salt pan (geology)">salt pan</a>, lies in the north.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Limpopo_River" title="Limpopo River">Limpopo River</a> Basin, the major landform of all of southern Africa, lies partly in Botswana, with the basins of its tributaries, the <a href="/wiki/Notwane_River" title="Notwane River">Notwane</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bonwapitse_River" title="Bonwapitse River">Bonwapitse</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mahalapye" title="Mahalapye">Mahalapye</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lotsane_River" title="Lotsane River">Lotsane</a>, <a href="/wiki/Motloutse_River" title="Motloutse River">Motloutse</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Shashe_River" title="Shashe River">Shashe</a>, located in the eastern part of the country. The Notwane provides water to the capital through the <a href="/wiki/Gaborone_Dam" title="Gaborone Dam">Gaborone Dam</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Cuando_River" title="Cuando River">Chobe River</a> lies to the north, providing a boundary between Botswana and Namibia's <a href="/wiki/Zambezi_Region" title="Zambezi Region">Zambezi Region</a>. The Chobe River meets with the <a href="/wiki/Zambezi" title="Zambezi">Zambezi River</a> at a place called <a href="/wiki/Kazungula" title="Kazungula">Kazungula</a> (meaning a small <a href="/wiki/Sausage_tree" class="mw-redirect" title="Sausage tree">sausage tree</a>, a point where <a href="/wiki/Sebitwane" class="mw-redirect" title="Sebitwane">Sebitwane</a> and his <a href="/wiki/Makololo" class="mw-redirect" title="Makololo">Makololo tribe</a> crossed the Zambezi into Zambia).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity_and_conservation">Biodiversity and conservation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Biodiversity and conservation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_of_Botswana" title="Wildlife of Botswana">Wildlife of Botswana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cebras_de_Burchell_(Equus_quagga_burchellii),_vista_a%C3%A9rea_del_delta_del_Okavango,_Botsuana,_2018-08-01,_DD_28.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Cebras_de_Burchell_%28Equus_quagga_burchellii%29%2C_vista_a%C3%A9rea_del_delta_del_Okavango%2C_Botsuana%2C_2018-08-01%2C_DD_28.jpg/220px-Cebras_de_Burchell_%28Equus_quagga_burchellii%29%2C_vista_a%C3%A9rea_del_delta_del_Okavango%2C_Botsuana%2C_2018-08-01%2C_DD_28.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Cebras_de_Burchell_%28Equus_quagga_burchellii%29%2C_vista_a%C3%A9rea_del_delta_del_Okavango%2C_Botsuana%2C_2018-08-01%2C_DD_28.jpg/330px-Cebras_de_Burchell_%28Equus_quagga_burchellii%29%2C_vista_a%C3%A9rea_del_delta_del_Okavango%2C_Botsuana%2C_2018-08-01%2C_DD_28.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Cebras_de_Burchell_%28Equus_quagga_burchellii%29%2C_vista_a%C3%A9rea_del_delta_del_Okavango%2C_Botsuana%2C_2018-08-01%2C_DD_28.jpg/440px-Cebras_de_Burchell_%28Equus_quagga_burchellii%29%2C_vista_a%C3%A9rea_del_delta_del_Okavango%2C_Botsuana%2C_2018-08-01%2C_DD_28.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6213" data-file-height="4148" /></a><figcaption>Zebras roaming the Okavango Basin</figcaption></figure>
<p>Botswana has diverse areas of wildlife habitat. In addition to the delta and desert areas, there are <a href="/wiki/Grassland" title="Grassland">grasslands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Savanna" title="Savanna">savannas</a>, where <a href="/wiki/Blue_wildebeest" title="Blue wildebeest">blue wildebeest</a>, <a href="/wiki/Antelope" title="Antelope">antelopes</a>, and other mammals and birds are found. Northern Botswana has one of the few remaining large populations of the endangered <a href="/wiki/African_wild_dog" title="African wild dog">African wild dog</a>. <a href="/wiki/Chobe_National_Park" title="Chobe National Park">Chobe National Park</a>, found in the <a href="/wiki/Chobe_District" title="Chobe District">Chobe District</a>, has the world's largest concentration of <a href="/wiki/African_bush_elephant" title="African bush elephant">African elephants</a>. The park covers about 11,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (4,247&#160;sq&#160;mi) and supports about 350 species of birds.
</p><p>The Chobe National Park and <a href="/wiki/Moremi_Game_Reserve" title="Moremi Game Reserve">Moremi Game Reserve</a> (in the Okavango Delta) are major tourist destinations. Other reserves include the <a href="/wiki/Central_Kalahari_Game_Reserve" title="Central Kalahari Game Reserve">Central Kalahari Game Reserve</a> located in the Kalahari Desert in <a href="/wiki/Ghanzi_District" title="Ghanzi District">Ghanzi District</a>; <a href="/wiki/Makgadikgadi_Pans_National_Park" class="mw-redirect" title="Makgadikgadi Pans National Park">Makgadikgadi Pans National Park</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nxai_Pan_National_Park" title="Nxai Pan National Park">Nxai Pan National Park</a> are in <a href="/wiki/Central_District_(Botswana)" title="Central District (Botswana)">Central District</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Makgadikgadi_Pan" title="Makgadikgadi Pan">Makgadikgadi Pan</a>. Mashatu Game Reserve is privately owned, located at the confluence of the <a href="/wiki/Shashe_River" title="Shashe River">Shashe</a> and Limpopo Rivers in eastern Botswana. The other privately owned reserve is <a href="/wiki/Mokolodi_Nature_Reserve" title="Mokolodi Nature Reserve">Mokolodi Nature Reserve</a> near Gaborone. There are also specialised sanctuaries like <a href="/wiki/Khama_Rhino_Sanctuary" title="Khama Rhino Sanctuary">Khama Rhino Sanctuary</a> (for <a href="/wiki/Rhinoceros" title="Rhinoceros">rhinoceros</a>) and Makgadikgadi Sanctuary (for <a href="/wiki/Flamingo" title="Flamingo">flamingos</a>). They are both located in Central District.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:204px;max-width:204px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a height="133" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/78/Okavango_Delta%2C_Botswana_%282674364913%29.jpg/300px-Okavango_Delta%2C_Botswana_%282674364913%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/78/Okavango_Delta%2C_Botswana_%282674364913%29.jpg/400px-Okavango_Delta%2C_Botswana_%282674364913%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3008" data-file-height="2000" /></a></span></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Giraffe_crossing_a_road_in_Botswana.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Giraffe_crossing_a_road_in_Botswana.jpg/200px-Giraffe_crossing_a_road_in_Botswana.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="133" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Giraffe_crossing_a_road_in_Botswana.jpg/300px-Giraffe_crossing_a_road_in_Botswana.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Giraffe_crossing_a_road_in_Botswana.jpg/400px-Giraffe_crossing_a_road_in_Botswana.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3008" data-file-height="2000" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">The <a href="/wiki/Okavango_Delta" title="Okavango Delta">Okavango Delta</a> (top).<br />Giraffe crossing a road (bottom).</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Botswana faces two major <a href="/wiki/Natural_environment" title="Natural environment">environmental</a> problems, drought and <a href="/wiki/Desertification" title="Desertification">desertification</a>, which are heavily linked. Three-quarters of the country's human and animal populations depend on groundwater due to drought. <a href="/wiki/Groundwater" title="Groundwater">Groundwater</a> use through deep borehole drilling has somewhat eased the effects of drought. Surface water is scarce in Botswana and less than 5% of the agriculture in the country is sustainable by rainfall. In the remaining 95% of the country, raising livestock is the primary source of rural income. Approximately 71% of the country's land is used for communal grazing, which has been a major cause of the desertification and the accelerating soil erosion of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-rala.is_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rala.is-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since raising livestock has been profitable for the people of Botswana, they continue to exploit the land with dramatically increasing numbers of animals. From 1966 to 1991, the livestock population grew from 1.7 million to 5.5 million.<sup id="cite_ref-rala.is_68-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rala.is-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 64">&#58;&#8202;64&#8202;</span></sup> Similarly, the human population has increased from 574,000 in 1971 to 1.5 million in 1995, a 161% increase in 24 years. Over 50% of all households in Botswana own cattle, which is currently the largest single source of rural income. Rangeland degradation or desertification is regarded as the reduction in land productivity as a result of overstocking and overgrazing, or as a result of veld product gathering for commercial use. Degradation is exacerbated by the effects of drought and climate change.<sup id="cite_ref-rala.is_68-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rala.is-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Environmentalists report that the <a href="/wiki/Okavango_Delta" title="Okavango Delta">Okavango Delta</a> is drying up due to the increased grazing of livestock.<sup id="cite_ref-afrol.com_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-afrol.com-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The Okavango Delta is one of the major semi-forested wetlands in Botswana and one of the largest inland deltas in the world; it is a crucial ecosystem to the survival of many animals.<sup id="cite_ref-afrol.com_69-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-afrol.com-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Department of Forestry and Range Resources has already begun to implement a project to reintroduce indigenous vegetation into communities in Kgalagadi South, Kweneng North and Boteti.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> Reintroduction of indigenous vegetation will help reduce the degradation of the land. The <a href="/wiki/United_States_Government" class="mw-redirect" title="United States Government">United States Government</a> has also entered into an agreement with Botswana, giving them US$7 million to reduce Botswana's debt by US$8.3 million. The stipulation of the US reducing Botswana's debt is that Botswana will focus on more extensive <a href="/wiki/Conservation_biology" title="Conservation biology">conservation</a> of the land.<sup id="cite_ref-afrol.com_69-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-afrol.com-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The country had a 2018 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 9.13/10, ranking it 8th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme" title="United Nations Development Programme">United Nations Development Programme</a> claims that poverty is a major problem behind the <a href="/wiki/Overexploitation" title="Overexploitation">overexploitation</a> of resources, including land, in Botswana. To help change this the UNDP joined in with a project started in the southern community of Struizendam in Botswana. The purpose of the project is to draw from "indigenous knowledge and traditional land management systems". The leaders of this movement are supposed to be the people in the community, to draw them in, in turn increasing their possibilities to earn an income and thus decreasing poverty. The UNDP also stated that the government has to effectively implement policies to allow people to manage their own local resources and are giving the government information to help with policy development.<sup id="cite_ref-afrol1_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-afrol1-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Botswana" title="Politics of Botswana">Politics of Botswana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Botswana" title="Government of Botswana">Government of Botswana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mokgweetsi_E.K._Masisi,_President_of_the_Republic_of_Botswana.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Mokgweetsi_E.K._Masisi%2C_President_of_the_Republic_of_Botswana.jpg/170px-Mokgweetsi_E.K._Masisi%2C_President_of_the_Republic_of_Botswana.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="238" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Mokgweetsi_E.K._Masisi%2C_President_of_the_Republic_of_Botswana.jpg/255px-Mokgweetsi_E.K._Masisi%2C_President_of_the_Republic_of_Botswana.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Mokgweetsi_E.K._Masisi%2C_President_of_the_Republic_of_Botswana.jpg/340px-Mokgweetsi_E.K._Masisi%2C_President_of_the_Republic_of_Botswana.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2383" data-file-height="3336" /></a><figcaption>The current president, <a href="/wiki/Mokgweetsi_Masisi" title="Mokgweetsi Masisi">Mokgweetsi Masisi</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Botswana is Africa's oldest democracy. The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Botswana" title="Constitution of Botswana">Constitution of Botswana</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Rule_of_law" title="Rule of law">rule of law</a>, which protects the citizens of Botswana and represents their rights. The politics of Botswana take place in a framework of a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democratic</a> republic, whereby the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Botswana" title="President of Botswana">president of Botswana</a> is both <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> and <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>, and is elected by and accountable to the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Botswana" title="Parliament of Botswana">Parliament of Botswana</a>. <a href="/wiki/Executive_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Executive power">Executive power</a> is exercised by the government. <a href="/wiki/Legislative_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative power">Legislative power</a> is vested in both the government and the Parliament of Botswana. <a href="/wiki/2019_Botswana_general_election" title="2019 Botswana general election">The most recent election</a>, its eleventh, was held on 23 October 2019. Since independence was declared, the party system has been <a href="/wiki/Dominant_party_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Dominant party system">dominated by</a> the <a href="/wiki/Botswana_Democratic_Party" title="Botswana Democratic Party">Botswana Democratic Party</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Botswana was ranked as a "flawed democracy" and 30th out of 167 states in the 2021 <a href="/wiki/Democracy_Index" class="mw-redirect" title="Democracy Index">Democracy Index (The Economist)</a>, higher than <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a> and <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a>, and just below the <a href="/wiki/Czech_Republic" title="Czech Republic">Czech Republic</a>. This was the second highest rating in Africa, and highest ranking in continental Africa (only the offshore island nation of <a href="/wiki/Mauritius" title="Mauritius">Mauritius</a> bested its ranking). According to 2023 <a href="/wiki/V-Dem_Democracy_indices" class="mw-redirect" title="V-Dem Democracy indices">V-Dem Democracy indices</a> Botswana ranks as 75th electoral democracy worldwide and 12th <a href="/wiki/Democracy_in_Africa" title="Democracy in Africa">electoral democracy in Africa</a>. According to <a href="/wiki/Transparency_International" title="Transparency International">Transparency International</a>, Botswana is the least corrupt country in Africa and ranks just below Portugal and South Korea.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Judiciary">Judiciary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Judiciary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Gaborone,_Botswana_High_Court.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Gaborone%2C_Botswana_High_Court.jpg/220px-Gaborone%2C_Botswana_High_Court.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Gaborone%2C_Botswana_High_Court.jpg/330px-Gaborone%2C_Botswana_High_Court.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Gaborone%2C_Botswana_High_Court.jpg/440px-Gaborone%2C_Botswana_High_Court.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="1836" /></a><figcaption>High Court of Botswana</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Judiciary" title="Judiciary">judiciary</a> is independent of the executive and the legislature.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It consists of a typical British-style court system of local Magistrates Courts, a <a href="/wiki/High_Court_of_Botswana" title="High Court of Botswana">High Court</a> and a Court of Appeal. The High Court is a superior court of record with unlimited original jurisdiction to hear and determine any criminal, civil or constitutional cases under any law. Appeals can be heard by the Court of Appeal. The head of the High Court is the chief justice.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Court of Appeal is the highest and final court in the country and deals with appeals from the High Court and the Industrial Court. The head of the Court of Appeal is the judge president.
</p><p>Judges are appointed by the president of Botswana on the recommendation of the Judicial Services Commission.
</p><p>With regard to the legal profession, although the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Law_Society_of_Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Law Society of Botswana (page does not exist)">Law Society of Botswana</a> has been in existence since 1997,<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> there is still no clear indication in their registry of attorneys as to how certain demographics, such as women, have fared in the legal field.
</p><p><b>List of chief justices of Botswana</b><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations_and_military">Foreign relations and military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Foreign relations and military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Botswanatroopsboardplane.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Botswanatroopsboardplane.png/220px-Botswanatroopsboardplane.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Botswanatroopsboardplane.png/330px-Botswanatroopsboardplane.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Botswanatroopsboardplane.png/440px-Botswanatroopsboardplane.png 2x" data-file-width="1597" data-file-height="898" /></a><figcaption>Botswana soldiers board a <a href="/wiki/Botswana_Defence_Force" title="Botswana Defence Force">Botswana Defence Force</a> plane to Mozambique, July 2021.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Botswana" title="Foreign relations of Botswana">Foreign relations of Botswana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Botswana_Defence_Force" title="Botswana Defence Force">Botswana Defence Force</a></div>
<p>At the time of independence, Botswana had no armed forces. It was only after the <a href="/wiki/Rhodesia" title="Rhodesia">Rhodesian</a> and <a href="/wiki/South_African_Army" title="South African Army">South African armies</a> attacked the <a href="/wiki/Zimbabwe_People%27s_Revolutionary_Army" title="Zimbabwe People&#39;s Revolutionary Army">Zimbabwe People's Revolutionary Army</a> and <a href="/wiki/Umkhonto_we_Sizwe" class="mw-redirect" title="Umkhonto we Sizwe">Umkhonto we Sizwe</a><sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> bases respectively that the <a href="/wiki/Botswana_Defence_Force" title="Botswana Defence Force">Botswana Defence Force</a> (BDF) was formed in 1977.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> The president is <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-chief" title="Commander-in-chief">commander-in-chief</a> of the armed forces and appoints a defence council and the BDF currently consists of roughly 60,000 servicemen. In 2019, Botswana signed the UN <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons" title="Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons">treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following political changes in South Africa and the region, the BDF's missions have increasingly focused on prevention of <a href="/wiki/Poaching" title="Poaching">poaching</a>, <a href="/wiki/Emergency_management" title="Emergency management">preparing for disasters</a>, and foreign <a href="/wiki/Peacekeeping" title="Peacekeeping">peacekeeping</a>. The United States has been the largest single foreign contributor to the development of the BDF, and a large segment of its officer corps have received U.S. training. The Botswana government gave the United States permission to explore the possibility of establishing an Africa Command (<a href="/wiki/United_States_Africa_Command" title="United States Africa Command">AFRICOM</a>) base in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Human_rights">Human rights</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Human rights">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Botswana" title="Human rights in Botswana">Human rights in Botswana</a></div>
<p>Many of the indigenous <a href="/wiki/San_people" title="San people">San people</a> have been forcibly relocated from their land to reservations. To make them relocate, they were denied access to water on their land and faced arrest if they hunted, which was their primary source of food.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc1_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc1-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> Their lands lie in the middle of the world's richest <a href="/wiki/Diamond" title="Diamond">diamond</a> field. Officially, the government denies that there is any link to mining and claims the relocation is to preserve the wildlife and ecosystem, even though the San people have lived sustainably on the land for millennia.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc1_83-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc1-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> On the reservations they struggle to find employment, and <a href="/wiki/Alcoholism" title="Alcoholism">alcoholism</a> is rampant.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc1_83-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc1-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
On 24 August 2018 the UN Special Rapporteur on Minorities, Fernand de Varennes, issued a statement calling on Botswana "to step up efforts to recognise and protect the rights of minorities in relation to public services, land and resource use and the use of minority languages in education and other critical areas."
</p><p>Until June 2019, <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Botswana" title="LGBT rights in Botswana">homosexual acts</a> were illegal in Botswana. A Botswana High Court decision of 11 June of that year struck down provisions in the Criminal Code that punished "carnal knowledge of any person against the order of nature" and "acts of gross indecency", making Botswana one of twenty-two African countries that have either <a href="/wiki/Decriminalization" title="Decriminalization">decriminalised</a> or <a href="/wiki/Legalization" title="Legalization">legalised</a> homosexual acts.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_Botswana" title="Capital punishment in Botswana">Capital punishment</a> is a legal penalty for murder in Botswana, and executions are carried out by hanging.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Botswana Centre for Human Rights, Ditshwanelo, was established in 1993.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Botswana" title="Districts of Botswana">Districts of Botswana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sub-districts_of_Botswana" title="Sub-districts of Botswana">Sub-districts of Botswana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size noresize" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><span><img
