<p>Botswana's ten districts are:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Southern_District_(Botswana)" title="Southern District (Botswana)">Southern District</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/South-East_District_(Botswana)" title="South-East District (Botswana)">South-East District</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kweneng_District" title="Kweneng District">Kweneng District</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kgatleng_District" title="Kgatleng District">Kgatleng District</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Central_District_(Botswana)" title="Central District (Botswana)">Central District</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/North-East_District_(Botswana)" title="North-East District (Botswana)">North-East District</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ngamiland_District" class="mw-redirect" title="Ngamiland District">Ngamiland District</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kgalagadi_District" title="Kgalagadi District">Kgalagadi District</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Chobe_District" title="Chobe District">Chobe District</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ghanzi_District" title="Ghanzi District">Ghanzi District</a></li></ul>
<p>Botswana's councils created from urban or town councils are: <a href="/wiki/Gaborone" title="Gaborone">Gaborone City</a>, <a href="/wiki/Francistown" title="Francistown">Francistown</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lobatse" title="Lobatse">Lobatse Town</a>, <a href="/wiki/Selebi-Phikwe" title="Selebi-Phikwe">Selebi-Phikwe Town</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jwaneng" title="Jwaneng">Jwaneng Town</a>, <a href="/wiki/Orapa" title="Orapa">Orapa Town</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sowa,_Botswana" title="Sowa, Botswana">Sowa Township</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Botswana" title="Economy of Botswana">Economy of Botswana</a></div>
<p>Since independence, Botswana has had one of the fastest growth rates in per capita income in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Botswana has transformed itself from one of the poorest countries in the world to an upper middle-income country. GDP per capita grew from $1,344 in 1950 to $15,015 in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Although Botswana was resource-abundant, a good institutional framework allowed the country to reinvest resource-income in order to generate stable future income.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> By one estimate, it has the fourth highest <a href="/wiki/Gross_national_income" title="Gross national income">gross national income</a> at <a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">purchasing power parity</a> in Africa, giving it a standard of living around that of Mexico.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tree_map_export_2009_Botswana.jpeg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Tree_map_export_2009_Botswana.jpeg/220px-Tree_map_export_2009_Botswana.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="172" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Tree_map_export_2009_Botswana.jpeg/330px-Tree_map_export_2009_Botswana.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Tree_map_export_2009_Botswana.jpeg/440px-Tree_map_export_2009_Botswana.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="1106" data-file-height="864" /></a><figcaption>Graphical depiction of Botswana's product exports in 28 colour-coded categories</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_of_Botswana.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Botswana.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Botswana.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Botswana.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Botswana.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/GDP_per_capita_development_of_Botswana.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_of_Botswana.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>GDP per capita of Botswana, 1950 to 2018</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_(current),_%25_of_world_average,_1960-2012;_Zimbabwe,_South_Africa,_Botswana,_Zambia,_Mozambique.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/GDP_per_capita_%28current%29%2C_%25_of_world_average%2C_1960-2012%3B_Zimbabwe%2C_South_Africa%2C_Botswana%2C_Zambia%2C_Mozambique.png/220px-GDP_per_capita_%28current%29%2C_%25_of_world_average%2C_1960-2012%3B_Zimbabwe%2C_South_Africa%2C_Botswana%2C_Zambia%2C_Mozambique.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/GDP_per_capita_%28current%29%2C_%25_of_world_average%2C_1960-2012%3B_Zimbabwe%2C_South_Africa%2C_Botswana%2C_Zambia%2C_Mozambique.png/330px-GDP_per_capita_%28current%29%2C_%25_of_world_average%2C_1960-2012%3B_Zimbabwe%2C_South_Africa%2C_Botswana%2C_Zambia%2C_Mozambique.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/GDP_per_capita_%28current%29%2C_%25_of_world_average%2C_1960-2012%3B_Zimbabwe%2C_South_Africa%2C_Botswana%2C_Zambia%2C_Mozambique.png/440px-GDP_per_capita_%28current%29%2C_%25_of_world_average%2C_1960-2012%3B_Zimbabwe%2C_South_Africa%2C_Botswana%2C_Zambia%2C_Mozambique.png 2x" data-file-width="1055" data-file-height="700" /></a><figcaption>GDP per capita (current),&#160;% of world average, 1960–2012; Zimbabwe, South Africa, Botswana, Zambia, Mozambique</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20130823203723/http://www.mti.gov.bw/">Ministry of Trade and Industry of Botswana</a> is responsible for promoting <a href="/wiki/Business_development" title="Business development">business development</a> throughout the country. According to the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a>, economic growth averaged over 9% per year from 1966 to 1999. Botswana has a <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_economic_freedom" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by economic freedom">high level of economic freedom</a> compared to other African countries.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> The government has maintained a sound <a href="/wiki/Fiscal_policy" title="Fiscal policy">fiscal policy</a>, despite consecutive <a href="/wiki/Government_budget_deficit" class="mw-redirect" title="Government budget deficit">budget deficits</a> in 2002 and 2003, and a negligible level of <a href="/wiki/External_debt" title="External debt">foreign debt</a>. It earned the highest sovereign <a href="/wiki/Credit_rating" title="Credit rating">credit rating</a> in Africa and has stockpiled foreign exchange reserves (over $7 billion in 2005/2006) amounting to almost two and a half years of current imports.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The constitution provides for an independent judiciary, and the government respects this in practice. The legal system is sufficient to conduct secure commercial dealings, although a serious and growing backlog of cases prevents timely trials. The protection of intellectual property rights has improved significantly. Botswana is ranked second only to South Africa among sub-Saharan Africa countries in the 2014 International Property Rights Index.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While generally open to foreign participation in its economy, Botswana reserves some sectors for citizens. Increased foreign investment plays a significant role in the privatisation of state-owned enterprises. Investment regulations are transparent, and bureaucratic procedures are streamlined and open, although somewhat slow. Investment returns such as profits and dividends, debt service, capital gains, returns on intellectual property, royalties, franchise's fees, and service fees can be repatriated without limits.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Botswana imports refined petroleum products and electricity from South Africa. There is some domestic production of electricity from coal.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy">Energy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Energy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066933788">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Energy_in_Botswana" title="Energy in Botswana">Energy in Botswana</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Energy_in_Botswana&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<p><a href="/wiki/Energy_in_Botswana" title="Energy in Botswana">Energy in Botswana</a> is a growing industry with tremendous potential. However almost all Botswana's electricity is generated from coal.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> No <a href="/wiki/Petroleum_reserves" class="mw-redirect" title="Petroleum reserves">petroleum reserves</a> have been identified and all petroleum products are imported refined, mostly from <a href="/wiki/South_Africa" title="South Africa">South Africa</a>. There is extensive woody biomass from 3 to 10t / hectare.
</p><p>Recently, the country has taken a large interest in <a href="/wiki/Renewable_energy" title="Renewable energy">renewable energy</a> sources and has completed a comprehensive strategy that may attract investors in the wind, solar and biomass renewable energy industries.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Botswana's power stations include <a href="/wiki/Morupule_Thermal_Power_Station" title="Morupule Thermal Power Station">Morupule Power Stations</a> B(600 MW), and A (132 MW),<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Orapa_Power_Station" title="Orapa Power Station">Orapa Power Station</a> (90 MW) and <a href="/wiki/Phakalane_Power_Station" title="Phakalane Power Station">Phakalane Power Station</a> (1.3 MW). 
</p>
The <a href="/wiki/International_Renewable_Energy_Agency" title="International Renewable Energy Agency">International Renewable Energy Agency</a> (IRENA) undertook an evaluation of the national energy sector in 2021 and found that Botswana could meet 15% of its energy needs in 2030 from its indigenous solar, wind, and bioenergy resources.<sup id="cite_ref-Energy_in_Botswana_dabla-etal-2021_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Energy_in_Botswana_dabla-etal-2021-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Energy_in_Botswana_irena-2021_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Energy_in_Botswana_irena-2021-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Roads_in_Botswana" title="Roads in Botswana">Roads in Botswana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Puente_internacional_de_Kazungula.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Puente_internacional_de_Kazungula.jpg/290px-Puente_internacional_de_Kazungula.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="171" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Puente_internacional_de_Kazungula.jpg/435px-Puente_internacional_de_Kazungula.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Puente_internacional_de_Kazungula.jpg/580px-Puente_internacional_de_Kazungula.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2436" data-file-height="1438" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Kazungula_Bridge" title="Kazungula Bridge">Kazungula Bridge</a>, connecting Botswana and Zambia</figcaption></figure>
<p>During SONA 2020 summit it was announced that Botswana has a network of <a href="/wiki/Roads" class="mw-redirect" title="Roads">roads</a>, of varied quality and capacity, totalling about 31,747 kilometres (19,727&#160;mi). Of these, 20,000 kilometres (12,000&#160;mi) are paved (this is including 134 kilometres (83&#160;mi) of motorways.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> The remaining 11,747 kilometres (7,299&#160;mi) worth are unpaved. Road distances are shown in kilometers and speed limits are indicated in <a href="/wiki/Kilometers_per_hour" class="mw-redirect" title="Kilometers per hour">kilometers per hour</a> (kph) or by the use of the national speed limit (NSL) symbol. Some vehicle categories have various lower maximum limits enforced by <a href="/wiki/Speed_limits" class="mw-redirect" title="Speed limits">speed limits</a>, for example trucks.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Finance">Finance</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Finance">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>An array of financial institutions populates the country's financial system, with pension funds and commercial banks being the two most important segments by asset size. Banks remain profitable, well-capitalised, and liquid, as a result of growing national resources and high interest rates.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Bank_of_Botswana" title="Bank of Botswana">Bank of Botswana</a> serves as a <a href="/wiki/Central_bank" title="Central bank">central bank</a>. The country's currency is the <a href="/wiki/Botswana_pula" title="Botswana pula">Botswana pula</a>.
</p><p>Botswana's competitive banking system is one of Africa's most advanced.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag may need clarification or removal of jargon. (September 2010)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Generally adhering to global standards in the transparency of financial policies and banking supervision, the financial sector provides ample access to credit for entrepreneurs.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The Capital Bank opened in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> As of August&#160;2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, there are a dozen licensed banks in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> The government is involved in banking through state-owned financial institutions and a special financial incentives programme that is aimed at increasing Botswana's status as a financial centre.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Credit is allocated on market terms, although the government provides subsidised loans.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Reform of <a href="/wiki/Non-bank_financial_institution" title="Non-bank financial institution">non-bank financial institutions</a> has continued in recent years, notably through the establishment of a single financial regulatory agency that provides more effective supervision.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> The government has abolished exchange controls, and with the resulting creation of new portfolio investment options, the <a href="/wiki/Botswana_Stock_Exchange" title="Botswana Stock Exchange">Botswana Stock Exchange</a> is growing.<sup id="cite_ref-botswana-facts-figs_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-botswana-facts-figs-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Gemstones_and_precious_metals">Gemstones and precious metals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Gemstones and precious metals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In Botswana, the Department of Mines<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> and Mineral Resources, Green Technology and Energy Security<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> led by Hon Sadique Kebonang in Gaborone, maintains data regarding mining throughout the country. <a href="/wiki/Debswana" title="Debswana">Debswana</a>, the largest <a href="/wiki/List_of_diamond_mines" title="List of diamond mines">diamond mining</a> company operating in Botswana, is 50% owned by the government.<sup id="cite_ref-botswana_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-botswana-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> The mineral industry provides about 40% of all government revenues.<sup id="cite_ref-worldbankbotswana_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-worldbankbotswana-108">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> In 2007, significant quantities of uranium were discovered, and mining was projected to begin by 2010. Several international mining corporations have established regional headquarters in Botswana, and prospected for diamonds, <a href="/wiki/Gold" title="Gold">gold</a>, <a href="/wiki/Uranium" title="Uranium">uranium</a>, <a href="/wiki/Copper" title="Copper">copper</a>, and even <a href="/wiki/Oil" title="Oil">oil</a>, many coming back with positive results. Government announced in early 2009 that they would try to shift their economic dependence on diamonds, over serious concern that diamonds are predicted to dry out in Botswana over the next twenty years.
</p><p>Botswana's <a href="/wiki/Orapa_diamond_mine" title="Orapa diamond mine">Orapa</a> mine is the largest diamond mine in the world in terms of value and quantity of carats produced annually.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Estimated to have produced over 11 million carats in 2013, with an average price of $145/carat, the Orapa mine was estimated to produce over $1.6 billion worth of diamonds in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-Kitco_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kitco-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Creative_industries">Creative industries</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Creative industries">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Increasing importance is being given to the economic contribution of the <a href="/wiki/Creative_industries" title="Creative industries">creative industries</a> to national economies. The <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Conference_on_Trade_and_Development" title="United Nations Conference on Trade and Development">United Nations Conference on Trade and Development</a> (UNCTAD) recompiles statistics about the export and import of goods and services related to the creative industries.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/World_Intellectual_Property_Organization" title="World Intellectual Property Organization">World Intellectual Property Organization</a> (WIPO) has assisted in the preparation of national studies measuring the size of over 50 copyright industries around the world.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> According to the WIPO compiled data, the national contribution of creative industries varies from 2% to 11% depending on the country.
</p><p>Using the WIPO-framework, the <a href="/wiki/Companies_and_Intellectual_Property_Authority" title="Companies and Intellectual Property Authority">Companies and Intellectual Property Authority</a>(CIPA) and the Botswana Institute for Development Policy Analysis were published by a sector-specific study in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> In 2016, copyright industries contributed 5.46% to value-added and 2.66% to the total labour force, 1.28% to exports, and 3.47% to imports.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Botswana" title="Demographics of Botswana">Demographics of Botswana</a></div>
<table class="wikitable" style="float: right; margin-right: 10px">
<caption>Population in Botswana<sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP-114">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-UN_WPP_2022_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</caption>
<tbody><tr>
<th scope="col" style="background:#cfb;">Year
</th>
<th scope="col" style="background:#cfb;">Million
</th></tr>
<tr>
<td>1950</td>
<td style="text-align:right;">0.4
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td>2000</td>
<td style="text-align:right;">1.7
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td>2020</td>
<td style="text-align:right;">2.4
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>As of 2012, the <a href="/wiki/Tswana_people" title="Tswana people">Tswana</a> are the majority ethnic group in Botswana, making up approximately 79% of the population, followed by <a href="/wiki/Kalanga_people" title="Kalanga people">Kalanga</a> at 11% and <a href="/wiki/San_people" title="San people">the San (Basarwa)</a> at 3%. The remaining 7% is made up of <a href="/wiki/White_people_in_Botswana" title="White people in Botswana">White Batswana</a>/European Batswana,<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Indians_in_Botswana" title="Indians in Botswana">Indians</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-cia_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> and a number of other smaller Southern African ethnic groups.
</p><p>Native groups include the <a href="/wiki/Bayei" class="mw-redirect" title="Bayei">Bayei</a>, Bambukushu, <a href="/wiki/Basubia" class="mw-redirect" title="Basubia">Basubia</a>, Baherero and <a href="/wiki/Kgalagadi_people" title="Kgalagadi people">Bakgalagadi</a>. The Indian minority is made up of both recent migrants and descendants of Indian migrants who arrived from <a href="/wiki/Mozambique" title="Mozambique">Mozambique</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kenya" title="Kenya">Kenya</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tanzania" title="Tanzania">Tanzania</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mauritius" title="Mauritius">Mauritius</a> and <a href="/wiki/South_Africa" title="South Africa">South Africa</a>.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Botswana_2016.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Botswana_2016.png/220px-Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Botswana_2016.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="153" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Botswana_2016.png/330px-Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Botswana_2016.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Botswana_2016.png/440px-Bev%C3%B6lkerungspyramide_Botswana_2016.png 2x" data-file-width="610" data-file-height="425" /></a><figcaption>Population pyramid 2016</figcaption></figure>
<p>Since 2000, because of deteriorating economic conditions in <a href="/wiki/Zimbabwe" title="Zimbabwe">Zimbabwe</a>, the number of <a href="/wiki/Zimbabweans_in_Botswana" title="Zimbabweans in Botswana">Zimbabweans in Botswana</a> has risen into the tens of thousands.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Fewer than 10,000 <a href="/wiki/San_people" title="San people">San people</a> are still living their traditional <a href="/wiki/Hunter-gatherer" title="Hunter-gatherer">hunter-gatherer</a> way of life. Since the mid-1990s the central government of Botswana has been trying to move San out of their historic lands.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The reason for this is unclear. (June 2023)">why?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p><a href="/wiki/James_Anaya" title="James Anaya">James Anaya</a>, as the Special Rapporteur on the situation of human rights and fundamental freedoms of indigenous people for the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> in 2010, described loss of land as a major contributor to many of the problems facing Botswana's indigenous people, citing the San's eviction from the <a href="/wiki/Central_Kalahari_Game_Reserve" title="Central Kalahari Game Reserve">Central Kalahari Game Reserve</a> (CKGR)<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> as a special example.<sup id="cite_ref-Anaya_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Anaya-120">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 2">&#58;&#8202;2&#8202;</span></sup> Among Anaya's recommendations in a report to the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Council" title="United Nations Human Rights Council">United Nations Human Rights Council</a> was that development programs should promote, in consultation with indigenous communities such as the San and Bakgalagadi people, activities in harmony with the culture of those communities such as traditional hunting and gathering activities.<sup id="cite_ref-Anaya_120-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Anaya-120">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 19">&#58;&#8202;19&#8202;</span></sup>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1148739982">.mw-parser-output .largestCities-table-background{background:#f9f9f9}.mw-parser-output .largestCities-cell-background{background:#f0f0f0}</style>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Botswana" title="Languages of Botswana">Languages of Botswana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Setswana" class="mw-redirect" title="Setswana">Setswana</a></div>
<p>The official language of Botswana is English, while <a href="/wiki/Tswana_language" title="Tswana language">Setswana</a> is widely spoken across the country.<sup id="cite_ref-gov.bw_languages_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gov.bw_languages-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> In Setswana, <a href="/wiki/Prefix" title="Prefix">prefixes</a> are more important than they are in many other languages, since Setswana is a <a href="/wiki/Bantu_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Bantu language">Bantu language</a> and has noun classes denoted by these prefixes. They include <i>Bo</i>, which refers to the country, <i>Ba</i>, which refers to the people, <i>Mo</i>, which is one person, and <i>Se</i> which is the language. For example, the main ethnic group of Botswana is the Tswana people, hence the name Botswana for its country. The people as a whole are Batswana, one person is a Motswana, and the language they speak is Setswana.
</p><p>Other languages spoken in Botswana include <a href="/wiki/Kalanga_language" title="Kalanga language">Kalanga</a> (Sekalanga), <a href="/wiki/Tshwa_language" title="Tshwa language">Sarwa</a> (Sesarwa), <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ndebele_language" title="Northern Ndebele language">Ndebele</a>, Kgalagadi, Tswapong, <a href="/wiki/Taa_language" title="Taa language">!Xóõ</a>, Yeyi, and, in some parts, <a href="/wiki/Afrikaans_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Afrikaans language">Afrikaans</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Botswana&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Botswana" title="Religion in Botswana">Religion in Botswana</a></div>
<div class="barbox tright" style="overflow-x: auto;">
<div style="border:1px solid silver; font-size:88%; padding:0.4em; width:auto; background: white;">
<table style="text-align:left; border-collapse:collapse; width:100%;">
<tbody><tr style="background:#ddd"><th style="text-align:center;" colspan="5"><a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Botswana" title="Religion in Botswana">Religion in Botswana</a> (<a href="/wiki/Pew_Research_Center" title="Pew Research Center">Pew Research</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-pew_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pew-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup></th></tr>
<tr style="font-size:88%; height:4px;">
<td colspan="2" style="padding:0 4px; text-align:left;">religion</td>
<td style="width:100px; text-align:left;"></td>

<tr>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;"><a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Botswana" title="Christianity in Botswana">Protestant</a></td>
